## 2026-10-13 continuation — turn 1/2 bounded change

- `e2bf3aa`: corrected runner lifecycle bug. Do NOT clear the RAF queue or reset mock time between successive battles; the real core owns its RAF handle and would otherwise retain a dead handle.
- `2a345b3`: `v3/legacy-sim-core.js` Turtle melee contact reach is bounded below by opposing visual collision radii minus the existing core's distance discount, with 0.002 contact tolerance. This corrects a geometric gap between Turtle nominal 27/500 attack reach and two-heavy contact separation. No HP drain, side teleport, Ultimate rebalance or sweeping recovery changes.
- `01a7f75`: isolated `.github/workflows/qa-duels.yml` tests ordered 300 duels, existing evolution/determinism and Ultimate abilities, uploading a JSON receipt on completion. Restricted to this QA feature branch; no deployment action.

Static explanation: turtle visual collision radius is `(.014+14/1300)*1.34≈.03320`; opposing pair spacing ≈.06639; old attack effective reach is `.054+(.02477*2*.22)≈.06490` (less than separation). With corrected contact-aware reach it is ≥ separation plus a small tolerance. Existing `reengageStep`/`recoverDeadlock` definitions remain uncalled; do not turn on a global recovery/balance change without new reproduction.

## Turn 4B bounded cache checkpoint — 2026-10-14
This packet introduces a revision-aware **image cache only**, not a claim of atomic offline application releases.

Changed files / commits:
- `290d412` `sw.js`: cache namespace `showdown-lab-stream4b1`. Cache-first **only** same-origin `/v3/assets/**` WebP/PNG/JPEG with explicit `?v=` revision. HTML, modules, other requests remain network-first. Cache only HTTP 200 successful responses, never failed/partial responses; offline previously cached same-version image/modules/navigation fallback; previous Showdown Lab cache namespaces deleted on activation. Existing shell remains mandatory at install.
- `09887d7` `tools/check-launch-guide.cjs`: scoped CacheStorage test mock updated for new service worker.
- `1fa9734` `tools/qa/check-cache4b.cjs`: VM service worker runtime tests for cache-first versioned image, revision change/new URL fetch, network-first JS, offline cached JS/navigation, no caching 404, failed mandatory install, previous-namespace cleanup.
- `bbf818f` `.github/workflows/qa-duels.yml`: execute cache 4B and launch-guide compatibility checks in CI.

GitHub Actions [run 38513313594](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38513313594) on tested head `bbf818f0f87de6d0bc25e74453e03e8fd37cbe30`: **SUCCESS** for node-regressions (300 ordered duels + Turn 4A stream + Turn 4B cache + legacy Guide/SW + evolution/Ultimate) and browser-turn3 (Chromium viewport/Dragon/Knight/phone touch). Independent Knight candidate art job was not required for cache acceptance and was in progress at the time of verification.

Open high-risk conditions: `skipWaiting`/activation deletes prior cache, and executable modules are fetched individually. Therefore no atomic shell+module consistency guarantee exists across interrupted deployment, active old clients, or offline upgrade. Do not claim Turn 4 is fully complete; atomic/versioned release bundle, interrupted upgrade, explicit offline readiness per selected fighter pack, slow network and memory/device metrics require a separate packet and additional QA. Global `vercel.json` no-store was deliberately **not** changed. No production deployment, no main merge, PR #14 DRAFT. Do not deploy without release authorization.

## Turn 4C staged release checkpoint — 2026-10-14
**Changes (all on draft PR #14; no main merge or production deployment):**
- `e5bc9f1` `sw.js`: release-specific cache `showdown-lab-release4c1`; stage root and /v3 shells plus 41 HTML-listed CSS/JS dependencies in a single install `cache.addAll`. A failed dependency causes worker installation to fail and discards the partial new cache; old installed worker remains available. Crucially no `skipWaiting`, so open old tabs continue with their prior worker until normal browser activation. Once activated, remove older Showdown Lab namespaces (not unrelated app caches). Installed shell/core modules served from their own matching cache; versioned optional art fetched on demand and cached first.
- `50f08e6` `tools/check-launch-guide.cjs`: Guide/HTML checks decoupled from previous network-first-only service worker fixture.
- `7c41964` `tools/qa/check-release4c.cjs`: fresh test for full core-list parity with HTML, staged installation, rollback of an interrupted module fetch, waiting-worker retention, offline shell and core, versioned optional sprite and invalid module behavior.
- `080f29a` `.github/workflows/qa-duels.yml`: replaces Turn 4B SW contract step with Turn 4C staged-release contract test.
- `a3d9844` `sw.js`: cache both canonical Home portraits as mandatory offline assets, and explicitly reject unlisted `/v3/*.js|css` modules rather than fetching an incompatible module from a newer release.
- `f86f8ba`, `09f5766` `tools/qa/check-release4c.cjs`: assert offline Home portrait and correct staged resource count. Mandatory install now comprises 2 HTML shells, icons/manifest, 2 Home portraits and 41 JavaScript/CSS resources.

**Verified evidence:** GitHub Actions [run 38513711174](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38513711174), source commit `09f5766f5dc4b0a27f7baa695fa59dc400bac67e`, all **3 jobs successful**. Node regression steps include 300/300 ordered duels, evolution/deterministic checks, Turn 3, Turn 4A, new Turn 4C staged shell contract, existing Guide and ten Ultimate abilities. Browser-turn3 revalidates three phone-sized Chromium viewports and approved Knight/Dragon rendering; Knight candidate job succeeded.

**Important limits:** The current service-worker design is staged/activation-safe, but **not proven cryptographically content-addressed**. A server deployment changing unversioned or stale-query module contents midway through installation can still stage a mixed snapshot. The generated literal runtime list must be synchronized with HTML changes (enforced by QA). The tests here simulate offline/install transitions in Node; they do not replace a real service-worker browser interrupted-update, cold/slow-network bandwidth receipt or iPhone Safari PWA device acceptance. Offline readiness for user-selected non-Home fighter bundles is still not exposed in the UI. Full Turn 4 is therefore not yet accepted; next gate is physical browser SW lifecycle, offline readiness and content-revision strategy. Continue without touching combat or generating new art.

## Turn 4F — SHA-256 pinned core bundle + HTTP-200 tamper rejection — 2026-10-14
Source-only draft PR #14 checkpoint; no main merge or production deploy.
- `1d8e760` `tools/qa/release-hashes.cjs`: Node crypto SHA-256 generator for root HTML, /v3 HTML and all 41 HTML-listed JS/CSS. `--verify` checks committed `RELEASE_SHA256` against actual source bytes and HTML module order, fails on stale digest/changed file.
- `9d3bf0c` `sw.js`: **43 pinned SHA-256 entries** embedded in release SW; after staging entire critical shell bundle, compute Web Crypto SHA-256 on each cached response, reject and discard candidate cache on mismatch, preserve old worker. Validation occurs *before* new service-worker install resolves and does not use a mutable network integrity document. The existing opt-in fighter cache API and authored art remain untouched.
- `5e9a9f5` `tools/qa/check-release4c.cjs`: mocked CacheStorage now holds actual repository bytes and Web Crypto; verifies both successful integrity install and rejected HTTP-200 altered module bytes with old cache retention.
- `151e0be` `.github/workflows/qa-duels.yml`: SHA-256 `--verify` is a required pre-duel CI gate.
- `e7dddb5` `tools/qa/check-upgrade-browser.cjs`: adversarial real Chromium test changes the new worker's release identity and serves a **200 OK but tampered JavaScript file** at an expected module URL; confirms candidate installation rejection, old worker/cache survive and old Home reloads offline.
- **Verified result:** [GitHub Actions run 38518066385](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38518066385), tested source commit `e7dddb5e18725f6946d4de5a6396175d8f2ef840`: all **five** CI jobs SUCCESS (node suite with pinned-hash guard, 300/300 duels and past evolution/Ultimate/stream/Guide tests; standard phone-size Chromium art/touch; actual cold/warm/offline plus Turtle pack and throttling; HTTP-200 tampered module upgrade recovery; Knight art candidate generation).

Integrity qualification: complete shell and 41 dependency bytes verified against pinned SHA-256 at SW installation. This guards against staggered content changes *during* staged install, even when server returns status 200. It is **not** full content-addressed/immutable object retention at the CDN and does not cryptographically authenticate the SW script itself. Physical Safari iPhone 14 Pro Max device acceptance and live Vercel/CDN deployment rollback remain open. Future updates MUST regenerate hashes and change the release cache revision; CI now fails if a required source changes without matching pinned hashes. No production deployment authorized.

Recommended next: keep Turn 4 as implementation-complete with environment/device signoff pending, then create isolated Turn 5 renderer instrumentation/perf packet. Do not assert real iPhone acceptance until tested.

## Turn 5C — paired deterministic large-battle CPU envelopes (2026-10-14)

**Implementation:** `7852ea0` adds `tools/qa/check-stress5c.cjs`. It runs the actual v3 LegacySimCore inside isolated Node VM environments with fake RAF timestamps and identical 10-fighter roster composition, mixes Base/Evolved/Ultimate ranks, seed, 1x speed and 90 frames. It tests 80 and 120 total fighters × seeds 1337/7301/27919, comparing two variants of the **same current source**: optimized and an in-memory reconstruction of the original `nearest` pressure scan plus repeated `separate` radius calls (no tracked gameplay fork). Every 15 frames it SHA-256 fingerprints tick, winner, all units' team/type/HP/positions/targets/actions/cooldowns/burrow/ward. Exact fingerprints and end ticks are asserted equal for each matched scenario, with no core errors. It records wall-clock mean, p50, p95, max and summed RAF callback durations (wall-clock VM overhead included). `640c8db` adds CI mandatory stress run and artifact alongside 300-duel receipt. Runtime modules and pinned release SHA-256 hashes are untouched by this test-only change.

**CI evidence:** [Actions run 38525964614](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38525964614) on source commit `640c8dbe857447e2cf48b8c6875798397e28606d`: **5/5 jobs SUCCESS**. 300/300 ordered duels, six stress parity scenarios, Guide/Ultimate checks, static art, Chromium 430px portraits and swarm, actual cached offline selected fighter, broken update recovery, source/manifest integrity all pass.

**Exact test receipts:**
- 80 units seed 1337: original 776.98ms vs optimized 426.37ms over 90 callbacks, speedup 1.82x; per-frame p95 9.33→7.68ms.
- 80 units seed 7301: 752.21→401.79ms, speedup 1.87x; p95 9.64→6.19ms.
- 80 units seed 27919: 757.15→426.97ms, speedup 1.77x; p95 9.45→6.86ms.
- 120 units seed 1337: 2049.65→888.11ms, speedup 2.31x; p95 24.52→13.83ms.
- 120 units seed 7301: 2106.72→895.48ms, speedup 2.35x; p95 26.08→13.32ms.
- 120 units seed 27919: 2010.57→865.71ms, speedup 2.32x; p95 25.26→13.27ms.
- Averages: 80 fighters original 762.11ms vs optimized 418.38ms (1.82x); 120 fighters original 2055.65ms vs optimized 883.10ms (2.33x). These are paired deterministic **simulation-only** wall-clock measurements on GitHub-hosted Node runner, not production iPhone FPS, main-thread browser total or GPU time. Avoid extrapolating identical relative improvement at lower fighter counts.

**Decision:** Keep the low-risk `nearest` + `separate` micro-optimizations, backed by deterministic parity and meaningful CPU savings at high density. No further fighter art degradation, collision shortcut, targeting pressure approximation or gameplay balance adjustment justified. Next Turn 5D: profile and optimize `CombatStaging.prepare` via separate browser-only instrumented staging work; validate 80/120 *on-screen* arrangements at 430px portrait and compare spacing, cropping, overlap and frame costs. Device Safari signoff and production deployment are still pending. The branch and PR #14 remain draft/unmerged.

## Turn 5E — high-density cutoff assessment, bounded visual spread and real 120-fighter browser QA (2026-10-14)

**Decision on 60-fighter limit:** The cutoff is **not load-bearing for combat simulation or hitboxes**. It appears only in `CombatStaging.limits()`, an independent presentation-offset module; `v3/camera.js` consults the same limits to reserve image space. The original `count<=60` limit guarded the four-pass O(n²) allied-pair overlap solver, preventing excessive staging CPU cost for large armies. Removing that limit naively would be harmful.

**Implemented on QA branch:**
- `173abea` `v3/combat-staging.js`: keep unchanged full staged pair resolution for ≤60; for >60, use a reduced max offset (x=min(34px,10% arena width), y=min(23px,7% arena height)), local grid neighbour fanning and smooth easing, **without** O(n²) pair correction passes. Only visual offsets, no simulation coordinates or combat rule changes. Camera fitting uses the new limits automatically.
- `7c0477d` `tools/qa/check-staging5d.cjs`: strict prior-implementation geometry equivalence retained for ≤60 fighters; >60 intentional visual differences tested separately.
- `989b599`, `64cda8d` `tools/qa/check-dense5e.cjs`: paired disabled-vs-bounded dense visual spread test at 61,80,100,120,160 fighters × 3 seeded fixtures, 28 animated frames each; asserts displacement limits, no out-of-arena anchor points, reduced aggregate allied centre proximity and broad occlusion.
- `3bbb03a` `.github/workflows/qa-duels.yml`: required dense test with JSON artifact. `68adf0f`/`311e501` both HTML entrypoints point at `combat-staging.js?v=dense5e1`; `4e44d71` SW update to `showdown-lab-release5e1`. `b6b9213` pinned matching 43 SHA-256 shell/module hashes; `52e1829` restored mandatory integrity gate.
- `63f26ce` `tools/qa/check-turn3-browser.cjs`: real Chromium QA programmatically starts 120-fighter battle at 430px portrait; saves `dense120-430.png`, counts real visual-envelope bounding-box intersections, close allied centre overlaps and clipping. `f657363`: follow-on 90-frame profiling at 120 active fighters, with median/p95 RAF interval and draw cost.

**Verified:** [GitHub Actions run 38529127230](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38529127230), tested commit `f65736361c3ac340dc3c7686cf5173c10f1fc932`, **5/5 CI jobs SUCCESS**. SHA-256 release integrity, 300/300 ordered duels, six 80/120 simulator comparisons with exact parity, nine ≤60 staging geometry fixtures, 15 >60 visual-spread fixtures, mobile screen/Dragon/Knight QA, selected L4 offline art, and staged SW corruption/recovery all passed.

**Quantitative evidence:**
- In synthetic 61–160 fighter fixtures, bounded presentation fanning reduced close allied centre-overlap pairs to **0.8533× baseline** (~14.7% fewer) and broader near-body occlusion proxies to **0.8575×** (~14.3% fewer) aggregated across seeds/sizes; 0 out-of-bounds anchors. These are simplistic synthetic geometry proxies, not human-perceived silhouette clarity.
- Real browser 120-unit run: `count=120`, `clipped=0` according to fighter visual envelopes, `closeOverlap=345`, `boxesIntersect=1154`, camera arena x=14, y≈346, width≈410, height≈493. Actual crowding remains high; change **does not solve** 120-fighter readability. Screenshot is attached to `browser-turn3` CI artifact.
- Real Chromium 120-unit 90-frame sample: frame interval p50≈**33.3 ms**, p95≈**50 ms**, 61/90 intervals >33.3 ms; main-thread draw p95≈**3.6 ms**. This suggests pacing/bottleneck beyond `draw()` and is **not physical iPhone Safari performance**.
- Staging-only Node VM cost for 120 bodies across 28 frames ~86–96 ms (new) vs ~13 ms (disabled old), so fanning is materially more expensive than disabling presentation movement. It avoids the much costlier four-pass overlap solver but is not CPU-free. No direct same-run browser A/B demonstrates the net FPS cost attributable to staging alone.

**Release judgment:** Keep as a candidate confined to draft PR #14, **do not deploy**. Cutoff is a presentation-performance safety choice, not a mechanical necessity; the bounded dense path improves geometric proxies with modest staging cost but has insufficient visual clarity and unsatisfactory measured 120-fighter frame pacing for production acceptance. Next Turn 5F should optimize dense staging neighbour selection and/or adaptive quality, test camera composition/lane separation and real visual silhouettes, and compare same-seed 120-fighter browser A/B under matched conditions. Do not fix crowding by shrinking all sprites or hiding effects. Device Safari signoff still pending.

## Turn 5F — frame-local staging caches, large-battle parity and next visual constraints (2026-10-14)

Work confined to `qa-turn1-duel-baseline` draft PR #14; main/production unchanged.

## Turn 5G — dense depth-lane fighter stacking and QA (2026-10-14)

Candidate reduced occupied visual area at high density, with no convincing corresponding legibility benefit in visual inspection.

**Decision: reject the .80→.74 multiplier for runtime integration.** Keep current `v3/camera.js`, staging, SW integrity manifest and gameplay unchanged. The QA A/B fixture remains useful. Evaluate per-sprite alpha visibility before any gameplay changes. Do not incorrectly claim that the proxy validated a solution.

## Turn 5H — actual authored alpha-visibility evidence (2026-10-14)

Each scenario records `alphaVisibilityRatio`, `recognizableProxy` (# units with ≥45% of their own drawn nontransparent pixels not overpainted), and `mostlyHidden` (# units with <15% visible). The mask is based on `FighterAssets.draw` including effects/halos and is therefore a **sampled alpha occupancy proxy**, not semantic readability or exact anatomy-only matte. Maintains identical camera/staging, real 5G painter lanes, 430×932 Chromium, and 20/40/60/80/120 synthetic or live120 fixtures; compares baseline vs guarded staging and a distinct visual-only wider-formation proxy. The apparent pixel improvement supports further actual formation candidate evaluation but does not verify combat balance, world positions or stage effect.

### Verified engagement persistence result and decision

- [Run 38580132838](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38580132838): **6/6 CI jobs passed** (node 300-duel/evolution stress, Chromium authored sprites, PWA offline/upgrade, art). This verifies the new read-only diagnostics, not tri-zone game readiness.

Baseline vs ±.26 vs ±.32: first detectable damage **frame 65.0 / 71.7 / 75.0**, active sectors at frame360 **2.56 / 2.67 / 2.56 of 3**. Spawn same-side nearness **113.67 / 95.89 / 86.56**. Thus larger deployment separation improves initial packing, but at this sampling point **does not create more simultaneously active sectors** and delays first damage ~10 frames (~0.17s at nominal 60fps) for the wide variant. Sampling is 15-frame coarse; do not treat as precise attack timings.
- **Decision:** do not promote spawn-only tri-zone changes to runtime. Need experiment with locality-aware target selection, squad cohesion/engagement slots and controlled flanking (no rigid zone boundaries), plus full outcome, role and actual sprite QA. Crucially, preserve small-fight baseline and validate against enemy target clustering/stranded fighters. The `check-zone-engagement.cjs` diagnostic and machine-readable evidence remain permanent regression research tools; active QA branch only, no merge/deploy.

## Soft locality-aware targeting investigation — 2026-10-14

- Created isolated `tools/qa/check-zone-locality.cjs` (commit `8ec1617`), derived from the prior three-zone persistence profiler, with four VM-only source variants: original baseline, spawn-only zones32, and zones32 plus *soft* cross-sector target-score penalties of .08 or .16 when target and actor differ in Y by >.22. Existing nearest-target distances, pressure penalty, free movement and attack rules remain intact. Unlike rigid lane fences, units can pursue across sectors where doing so remains optimal. No application/runtime code was modified.

It measures 40/80/120 combatants × 3 matched seeds, first sampled HP damage and active three-sector cross-team-proximity count after 360 frames. **Check CI completion and metrics before recommending either penalty.**
- Acceptance caveats: 15-frame HP sampling and .12-radius inter-team proximity are proxies, not direct attack events or real terminal outcomes. A higher locality penalty could strand survivors or change sniper/assassin roles; require longer-run, role-specific and mirrored terminal tests before game integration. If target-scoring alone fails to create separate fronts, investigate engagement-slot policies with guarded relaxation, not hard paths or walls. Keep runtime and main untouched.

For ordinary matches (<60), preserve exact baseline behavior if candidate is eventually made density-gated.

### October 14 — Beetank ram pose vs actual approach/contact

- User noticed Beetank may hold the ram pose while far away from an enemy. Confirmed directly from protected `beetankStep`: when `d > u.range/500`, `u.special<=0`, and `d<.34`, it sets `u.charge=26`; during charge it sets `u.action='ram'` and moves toward the target at 2× speed, while a real ram impact only occurs if `d < (u.range/500)*1.5`. Therefore a ram **action frame does not imply a hit**, and can include substantial approach movement.
- The one-seed mirrored evidence (103 baseline vs 104 experimental ram contacts, 531 vs 874 ram action frames) is consistent with more time charging but not more collisions. Commit `194750b` adds QA-only `ramApproachFrames` (ram pose while target remains beyond 1.5× nominal attack range) alongside total `ramFrames` and confirmed `beetankRamContacts`. This is sampled once per RAF and does not count charge activation events separately. This new metric **awaits CI**.
- The three-seed run 38615700147 had all non-damage required jobs passed when inspected, with damage-attribution-smoke still running. Do not infer full three-seed conclusion until it completes. No changes to visual sprites or combat logic yet; potential polish is to distinguish charging approach from hit impact without silently modifying gameplay.


### October 14 — three-seed evidence and spell-sigil rendering defect

- [Run 38615700147](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38615700147) passed ALL required jobs, including the three-seed `damage-attribution-smoke`: twelve matched scenario cases per variant (80/120, mirrored, seeds 1337/7301/8675309), terminal 12/12 for both, with winner divergence 1/12. Beetank actual ram impacts 287 baseline / 285 locality; Turtle shell reduction measured *before downstream mitigations* 10807.93 baseline / 9796.91 locality; Turtle effective reflection 301.547 baseline / 8.869 locality. These aggregate results are not normalized per deployment/exposure. The large reflection change merits investigation; the nearly identical ram contact counts do not support more effective Beetank charging from locality alone.
- [Run 38615999759](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38615999759) surfaced a separate `browser-turn3` error: `IndexSizeError: Failed to execute 'arc' on 'CanvasRenderingContext2D': The radius provided (-0.2125) is negative.` Identified a plausible vulnerable spell-sigil expression in `v3/v3.js`, `ctx.arc(0,0,rr-ring*7,...)`, where negative radius is possible for the inner rings. A candidate clamp was briefly committed but reverted in commit `82c9152` because the service-worker's SHA256 manifest pins the renderer bytes and must be updated atomically with any runtime change. Thus **no renderer fix is currently active**; this browser failure remains an explicit release blocker until root-cause confirmation, an atomic renderer/hash/cache update, and green browser/offline CI.
- Beetank QA now separately logs `ramApproachFrames` (visible ram pose while beyond impact threshold) alongside action frames and actual contacts; inspect the completed updated receipt before drawing animation conclusions. No production merge/deploy.


### October 14: canvas error investigation

CI run 38615999759 surfaced an intermittent negative-radius canvas error. The likely cause is the lightning particle radius expression `2.1*(1-age)` in `v3/v3.js`; an age greater than one makes the radius negative. A trial clamp was reverted in commit `40a7d21` because the release requires a synchronized integrity digest; repository writes to the offline manifest were blocked. The defect remains OPEN. Preserve existing pinned assets until renderer and manifest can be changed together and release/renderer/browser QA pass. The three-seed Beetank/Turtle profiler in run 38615700147 passed. Do not promote locality or deploy.

### October 14 — Assassin contact-path diagnostic

- Root-cause hypothesis from `legacy-sim-core.js`: Assassin prefers backline targets, seeks them via `nearest(u,true)`, uses `stalk` while out of nominal reach, teleports at <0.42 range when vanish is available, backstabs only at `d<=u.range/500` with cooldown ready, otherwise disengages. The experimental locality penalty disproportionately punishes vertically separated target choices; wide formations can make Assassin stalk without reaching a backstab opportunity. This is a hypothesis, not yet verified cause.
- Commit `07df70e` adds QA-only per-simulation Assassin action frame counts and target proximity/backline/exposure diagnostics, aggregated baseline vs locality across mirrored three-seed scenarios. Preserve the runtime core; no experimental target modifications or damage buffs until evidence confirms which phase fails. CI is pending for this instrumentation.
- Continue with contact opportunity, target distance and role-damage analysis; block locality rollout until Assassin effectiveness is restored. Beetank RAM comic contact gating and canvas particle radius fix are separately implemented with SHA-pinned offline release; check new CI rather than assuming prior successes prove this instrumentation.

### October 14 — milestone A implementation: actual contact components

At 25/50/75% of each match's actual observed lifetime, `WORLD CONTACT COMPONENTS` compares original, connected-Beetank and Swift Knight scenarios. This is more meaningful than the previous 3 horizontal bands, but still an instantaneous proximity proxy; no behavioural assumptions or stat promotions.

The 0.12 contact threshold is a measurement parameter, not the game's actual attack radius and may over-connect large fighters or ranged attacks; calibration/sensitivity study and role-specific reach remains on the backlog. Because grouping runs only on research snapshots, it cannot affect live simulation or gameplay CPU. **Do not confuse QA cost with production cost.** CI pending; no live core, renderer, root PWA, merge or deploy changes.

### October 14 — FIRST ACTUAL tactical director A/B: candidate rejected (verified)

- [CI 38652334936](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38652334936) `engagement-director-spike` SUCCESS; **the job passing establishes harness validity, not design acceptance**. Four 40/80-fighter paired matchups (one seed, mirror sides): control vs soft center director confirmed fighter-sourced hits **1530 vs 1511**, average occupied impact cells **16.50 vs 16.75**, mean sampled target switches **479.75 vs 457.50**, mean RAF callback counts **185.00 vs 182.25**, and one of four winners changed. Sniper attributed damage **13,774 vs 11,642** (~-15.5%), Barbarian **5918 vs 5050** (~-14.7%), Beetank **3744 vs 3360** (~-10.3%), Goblin **750 vs 451** (~-39.9%). Turtle improved **673 vs 1275**; Assassin **341 vs 505** but remains low absolute. This is a sizeable, mixed role redistribution without a meaningful demonstrated spatial-benefit gain. **REJECT this bounded soft-target penalty, do not integrate.** Runtime and existing fighters remain unmodified. Wall-clock total **8797 vs 9145 ms** across four cases (~+4%) is noisy, cannot infer iOS slowdown.
- Commit `9c3ebe6` adds explicit reproducible experimental verdict diagnostics: winner divergence, role effectiveness below 85% control retention and insufficient occupied-damage-cell footprint improvement trigger `REJECT_OR_REDESIGN` (soft research gate, not mandatory CI failure). The win divergence, Sniper and Goblin damage concerns independently warrant rejection.
- [Full confirmed hit CI 38651905349](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38651905349) subsequently completed successfully. Twelve 80/120-fighter mirrored cases, mean confirmed fighter hit-events within centered 25/50/75%-lifetime windows: original **248.0 / 110.92 / 41.58**, connected-Beetank **253.83 / 121.75 / 52.33**, raw Swift Knight **267.83 / 107.0 / 40.5**. Mean occupied impact grid cells original **16.33/14.83/10.92**, connected **16.0/15.92/11.33**. Attack counts/impact footprints are distinct from proximity group counts and visual readability; original wins early contact groups but connected has more middle/late registered hits. Neither is overall accepted as best. Ranged/Dragon continued contribution must be preserved.
- **Next direction:** a target-neutral *observer* or tactical context service should be tested first (calculate meaningful contacts and front lifecycle with **zero change** in targeting/hit/winners). Then prototype controlled *movement/guard/interception only* for Knight, selectively for Beetank, rather than a blanket proximity penalty. Profile and do deterministic parity before adding any world behavior. Presentation camera and FX work can proceed independently under its own budget. User-requested Sniper projectile/Dragon flame/ranged FX upgrade remains queued in ROADMAP.md for later; do not generate now.


### October 14 — new user design feedback: priority threats and virtual spell commander

- Added detailed design specification [`docs/THREAT_TARGETING_AND_COMMANDER_DESIGN.md`](THREAT_TARGETING_AND_COMMANDER_DESIGN.md) (`b25108a`) and active roadmap insertion (`298f028`). These are **future gameplay design specifications, not implementations**; the user asked to register ideas, not to start another blanket combat stat experiment immediately.
- Ranged and Assassin should choose the most damaging *reachable and comparably close* threat to allied infantry (e.g. Dragon over Knight while both in viable range), then switch defensively **before** an approaching Knight reaches a nearby ranged unit/squad, allowing actual Sniper aim/reload plus one or two shots when physically feasible. Predict time-to-contact, charge, ally threat, cooldown and viable escape; use hysteresis so no aim-reset churn. Assassin's flank/vanish mechanics require a specialized reach-and-risk policy, not a universal ranged rule.
- Use multi-target abilities when likely to produce maximum meaningful damage/disruption **without indefinite waiting**; preserve existing L4/L5 passive/proc mechanics and cooldowns. Dragon breath currently faces a single target then damages units in a geometric cone; special opportunity scoring should precede/fit its windup. Signature FX work for Sniper bullets/Dragon flame is still independently queued.
- Future *non-fighter* virtual commander policy: Manual/default, Auto Use Freely, Auto Tactical Reserve. Tactical reserve should hold BLOOM/AEGIS for real imminent losses and ARC/QUAKE for dangerous attackers or a genuine logjam; auto cannot bypass normal spell unlock/cooldown (separate existing Lab unlimited-spells option). Existing ARC/BLOOM core actions select the globally lowest absolute HP/HP fraction respectively; QUAKE/AEGIS are global. **Target-specific commands will require a separate core API extension and deterministic action scheduling**, not naive UI button automation. Expose via discreet battle menu or Lab subpage, protect manual override, settings, offline bundle, performance and fairness.
- Suggested next research: observer-only enemy threat scoring and Knight arrival-ETA trace against same-seed Dragon/Knight/Sniper fixtures, then isolated A/B with real all-role damage/retarget/shot-before-contact gates. This naturally follows the rejected proximity-center director, rather than retrying static locality penalties. No protected combat, renderer or production changes, no main merge/deploy.


### October 14 — user feedback: all-fighter tactical roles, swarm cooperation, and counterplay

- User wants the planned threat-targeting intelligence to extend beyond Sniper/Assassin: **tanks target large dangerous opponents as appropriate**, but retain protection duties; **swarms can fight hostile swarms or coordinate against isolated enemy melee fighters that would beat them one-on-one**. Every fighter should fill a meaningful gap and face a legitimate opponent counter, without deterministic rock-paper-scissors outcomes. Enemy danger, role advantage, **feasible contact**, nearby ally numbers and urgency must jointly affect selection.
- New canonical design document [ROLE_COUNTERPLAY_AND_TARGET_ECOLOGY.md](ROLE_COUNTERPLAY_AND_TARGET_ECOLOGY.md), commit `05bef95`: grounded all-ten-fighter role/counter matrix using current RC4 actions, L4/L5 Ultimates and Goblin Pack Ambush; distinguishes Knight interceptor, Beetank heavy disruptor and Turtle defender, Goblin coordinated pack vs heavy and hostile swarm, Assassin/Mole infiltration and anti-swarm AoE risks, plus ten specific QA acceptance fixtures, performance/replay and mobile fun gates.
- Linked into [THREAT_TARGETING_AND_COMMANDER_DESIGN.md](THREAT_TARGETING_AND_COMMANDER_DESIGN.md) at `f18adc7`, and active [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md) at `f0ddd07`. User's prior virtual commander with Manual / Auto Freely / Auto Tactical Reserve and planned polished authored Sniper bullets/Dragon flame remain intact and separate. No modifications to production simulator, targeting, visual effects, UI or deployment.
- Recommended **causally isolated** implementation sequence: observer-only per-role threat/reach trace, Sniper Dragon-vs-Knight with defensive ETA, Goblin pack-vs-heavy/hostile-pack fixtures, one tank role intercept/ram experiment, then remaining roles. Avoid an all-ten simultanous 'smart targeting' patch, generic giant-target bias, static matchup multiplier or blanket locality penalty (previous trial rejected). Test 1v1, 20/40/80/120, L1/L4/L5, matched mirrored seeds, hits/survival, target stability, actual pack proximity, diversity, real phone FPS and readability before any role goes live.

It returns *advisories* and scanning diagnostics. **It is not imported into the app or simulation and cannot change production targeting.** This directly advances the role/counter ecology design without repeating the rejected blanket locality penalty.
- Commit `1905985` adds `tools/qa/check-threat-observer.cjs` fixture suite: (1) Sniper chooses dangerous Dragon over similarly distant Knight, (2) switches to an approaching Knight before contact when useful, (3) ignores retreating Knight, (4) Goblins prefer a coordinated attack on an isolated heavy, (5) Goblins intercept opposing swarm threatening Sniper, (6) Beetank prefers reachable heavy enemy, (7) no target out of range. Test freezes synthetic inputs to prove observer purity. The harness also runs actual unchanged RC4 at 40/80/120 fighters with mirrored sides, compares entire final simulation states and every 15-frame trace checkpoint against a parallel run using only observational reads, and logs measured observer CPU costs. Fixture assertions are **proposed design behavior**, not claims about current live unit AI.
- Commit `6a79ba9` installs independent `threat-observer-parity` CI job with receipt artifact and Node syntax checks; isolated from `root-release-gate`. Commit `bc13530` memoizes per-opponent threat across fighters within an observer snapshot and computes approach ETA from *both* previous attacker and defender positions rather than assuming Sniper is stationary. Observer updates are intended to run at most every 12 logical ticks, not every rendered frame. Verify latest CI on optimized commit before declaring all checks passed.
- Quality/performance constraints: never compromise authored fighter silhouettes with procedural shading; future Sniper bullets, Dragon flame and ranged VFX must be authored/cached and accurately anchored to actual muzzle/mouth/impact. Maintain bright comic battle identity via readable real-event contact VFX, distinct roles, controlled impact typography and per-engagement FX priority rather than unbounded particles. Existing BAU rendering/profiling and FPS gates remain authoritative; observer must not increase production CPU because it is QA-only.
- **Next decision:** inspect independent fixture + deterministic parity + perf receipts; if green, prototype a Sniper-only targeting candidate in VM A/B with aim-reset, damage, opponent counterplay and 10× load tests. Do not integrate all ten roles at once. User's spell commander and later authored ranged FX remain queued separately. No merge/deploy.

### October 14 — takeover correctness hotfix: red-side victory accounting (QA branch only)

- **Source-verified P0 bug before fix:** `v3/simulation-adapter.js` had `const loser=w='blue'?'red':'blue';` inside `finish(w,us)`. The accidental assignment always evaluated the nonempty string as truthy, charged **red** one life irrespective of actual winner, and overwrote `w` as `'blue'`. Existing exhaustive duel QA checked *terminality* but did not assert that displayed match winner matched surviving team, so it could stay green. This is a deterministic match-economy/result bug, not a targeting/performance change.
- Fix commit [`846458b`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/846458b8285d22e97a2bf2cbfdb1ecdcb25d1276) changes to strict comparison (`w==='blue'`) and preserves `roundWinner` and `winner`. The `population-and-disruption` job now executes the test. **GitHub CI pending at documentation time; not yet a passed QA gate.** No change to RC4 damage, unit AI, formations or authored art.
- Root resource URL bumped to `simulation-adapter.js?v=winner-life1` at [`1b2e9d5`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/1b2e9d515dfda9c4c5cd8b738597ff5168b69d4d), worker atomic cache `showdown-lab-release-winnerlife1` and 42-entry SHA-256 pin map refreshed at [`9aa5e85`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/9aa5e85ab9fd384dcbc7afad46cd0dc47352bbb9). Pre-write hash recomputation matched prior root and adapter pins; new manifest and HTML dependency ordering matched in the write script. Require **CI root-release-gate and browser offline upgrade verification** before calling complete; do not merge to main/production.

### October 14/15 — collision fast-reject PROMOTED to QA runtime, pending full CI

**Change:** A direct A/B study isolated `separate()`'s highest-work pair scan. Its original `Math.hypot(dx,dy)` was performed for every pair on each 3–4-pass collision resolution even when fighters were too far apart to touch. The candidate computes the **exact same dynamic per-pair collision distance threshold**, then skips `Math.hypot` when `dx >= min || dx <= -min || dy >= min || dy <= -min`. Mathematically, any absolute coordinate gap ≥ `min` implies Euclidean distance ≥ `min`, so the skipped pair could never receive an impulse. Pair visitation order, 10-pass initial separation, live-state positions, all fallback math, RNG, attack timing and L4/L5 abilities remain untouched.

- **ABBA GitHub Node/VM result:** CI [38677396051](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38677396051) emitted **20 exact paired scenarios** (5 population × 2 mirrors × 2 speeds, with two control and two candidate executions each). All combat trace parity assertions passed before an **artifact-writing test bug** failed the job: the test guarded `QA_COLLISION_RECEIPT` but tried to write to undefined `QA_NEAREST_RECEIPT`. This was not a simulation, determinism or performance failure. Receipt log yielded candidate/control time ratios (lower better) by population: 40 **0.817**, 80 **0.753**, 120 **0.711**, 180 **0.696**, 300 **0.687** median across mirrored/speed samples. At 300 10×, mirror ratios **0.674 and 0.687**; ~31–33% synthetic Node/VM callback time reduction. These are **NOT actual Chrome/Safari FPS**. As with any microbenchmark, CPU, hardware and combat stage affect realized gain.
- Fixed QA output environment variable [`05c8fea`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/05c8feac79fc1fb9064e52ec27bc44628ea21c7f). **QA runtime change** [`add1a62`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/add1a62f6a679b54bc67b8cec6e95fe179aa07f9), root script cache-busting resource URL `legacy-sim-core.js?v=collision-fast1` [`2e7b57e`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/2e7b57ef262564176f2d972ef6499716ba86baf4), worker cache `showdown-lab-release-collisionfast1` with all 42 pinned SHA-256 digests [`9714e26`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/9714e26b24cfc17b7e7612f4d2ae8029b105c94f). Verified manifest recomputation against previous committed bytes, exact HTML/RUNTIME ordering and 42 entries during tool-side write.
- Updated isolated regression harness [`1d04e79`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/1d04e79a8fc8fb4018a05415c57fb3ccff6f6d35) to reconstruct the original unoptimized RC4 for its A/B control, comparing the *promoted* game code against original; retains exact multi-density mirrored/1×/10× per-callback trace gate. Replace ONLY in isolated source-variant A/B with a once-per-tick blue/red pressure map, **incremental target assignment and death updates** (including Assassin and Mole retargets), and `finally` cleanup; outside a tick retain original pressure scan for spells/deadlock. This preserves dynamic pressure semantics and prevents sampling a stale beginning-of-tick target cache.

- **New lightweight nearest-distance A/B research**: `tools/qa/check-distance-bound-spike.cjs` [`ec49bfe`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/ec49bfe8297220a2ee9c2592a5cf02a4bc189dea), independent `distance-bound-research` CI [`f230623`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/f230623a44d426fdeef1698c6f2befa683089d41), first run [38685870625](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38685870625) **queued** when documented. Within `nearest()`, after a current best score is known, prune a candidate only if `dx²+dy² >= (score+1e-12)²`, so a nonnegative-pressure-scored farther target cannot win. Retain actual `Math.hypot` and exact score for every feasible candidate, original pool/backliner ordering, action logic and RNG. Safety margin guards marginal rounding; this is still a hypothesis that requires CI bit-for-bit traces.
- Direct V8 40/120/300 short mirrors (including 300/10×) matched exact per-callback state in **8/8** cases, directional ratios **~0.93–0.96**. ABBA GitHub test repeats 40/80/120/180/300 at 1×/10× + mirrors and 12 longer seeded parity cases. **No live code, root HTML URL or offline hashes modified for this research.** If savings are inconsistent, reject it; if reliable, assess actual browser p95 and role counterplay before approval. No reduction to real fighter population or arbitrary display cap.


### October 15 — verified conservative nearest target bound promoted to QA branch (not production)

- **Evidence:** Independent [distance-bound CI 38685870625](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38685870625) completed **SUCCESS** with exact per-callback full combat-state parity for mirrored 40/80/120/180/300 1× and 10× cases, plus 12 longer mirrored/alternate-seed parity cases. ABBA median candidate/original synthetic callback time ratios: 40 **0.902**, 80 **0.858**, 120 **0.837**, 180 **0.796**, 300 **0.784**. Median observed improvement ~21.6% at 300 fighters; not an iPhone frame rate. All 300 size × speed × mirror ratios ~0.769–0.785. **Prior allocation-lean nearest rewrite remains rejected** as slower; these are different algorithms.
- **Proof of guarded equivalence:** Current target score `s = distance + max(0, pressure-2)*.035`, with strictly nonnegative pressure penalty. Once an existing best `score` is finite, a new target whose lower-bound Euclidean distance exceeds that score by `1e-12` cannot possibly win. The new source checks `dx²+dy² >= (score+1e-12)²` and bypasses `Math.hypot` only for those losing candidates. All remaining candidates use the **identical original `Math.hypot` and score calculation**; enemy eligibility, ordering, target switch pressure, Sniper/Dragon range, Assassin backliner preference and combat RNG untouched. Floating margin intentionally avoids equality/boundary error.
- **QA runtime integration:** Core change [`56ab00b`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/56ab00b59c0236ea3cdbecb94a7bb9117e95084d), root canonical `legacy-sim-core.js?v=target-bound1` [`3782421`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/378242182a9aef8e5832c53cd8cdb4d5508ebda3), service-worker `showdown-lab-release-targetbound1` 42 verified pins [`b7b3702`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/b7b3702f1cacd47fbadb5546fbd2105d5e98f727). Pre-write previous SHA256 pins matched existing source/root content, regenerated root/core hashes, verified manifest entries and HTML/script runtime list exactly match.
- Updated regression harness [`f2de795`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/f2de79527a60894dbb3589ecde6dc718d2578bca) reconstructs untouched original `nearest()` to compare it against **actual promoted** current core instead of requiring a nonexistent old source anchor. Post-integration [full CI 38686268917](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38686268917) **queued at this entry**. Must clear all active checks, especially 42-resource root/offline gates, 300 fighter stress, browser-density, actual role-effectiveness observers, mirrored duels. Do not call production or even branch release-ready until green.
- Separate **universal collision coarse-reach prefilter** is **QA-only** in `tools/qa/check-collision-coarse-bound-spike.cjs`, CI [38686077288](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38686077288) queued at writing. Direct V8 short 8/8 mirrored cases showed original state parity and roughly 18–20% further synthetic CPU savings at 300, but **no runtime change and no compound improvement claim**. Evaluate after target-bound1 post-CI and separately benchmark the candidate against the new target-bound baseline.
- Previous spatial-bin prototype and mutable target-pressure cache are held/rejected as poor return versus risk. Work prioritizes robust 300 fighter interaction, role contribution, visual readability and theatrical polish; real physical Safari performance is an open gate. Branch `qa-turn1-duel-baseline` only. **No merge/deploy.**

### October 15 — coarse collision reach prefilter integrated on QA; combat balance is next bounded phase

**User direction:** Continue CPU gains now, but explicitly return in a few turns to low damage by some fighters (Assassin and any other weak role), real simultaneous battles around the arena, meaningful counterplay, fun and rewarding comic theatre. Canonical [Combat Balance and Multi-front Return Plan](COMBAT_BALANCE_AND_MULTIFRONT_RETURN_PLAN_2026-10-15.md) added ([`c57410b`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/c57410b29290daeed60fae172bbaee18204a44d8)). B1 real attributed damage/utility/first-hit per role, B2 actual persistent multi-front fighting based on damage-positive engagement, B3 targeted role access/matchup (not blanket buffs), B4 player-visible effects/rewards. **Return immediately after the current coarse collision CI and one bounded portrait performance verification turn.**

**Coarse-bound1 performance implementation:** In `v3/legacy-sim-core.js`, `separate()` derives `globalBound=2*Math.max(...radii)*1.14`, the largest possible physical contact reach. For each ordered fighter pair, rejects on `|dx| >= globalBound` or `|dy| >= globalBound` before reading pair-specific team/radius. For every surviving pair, existing exact `min`, `Math.hypot`, orientation, force, mass, clamp, RNG and visit ordering are entirely preserved. Unlike expensive spatial bucket approach, this has no index building, stale data or sorts and no change to fighter count, attack, pressure, targets, abilities, levels or presentation.

- Committed core [`af6b421`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/af6b421087ec61865b69a94310b77ef7caa1532c), root resource `legacy-sim-core.js?v=coarse-bound1` [`ddcec5d`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/ddcec5db1eb139a1413e704d6a7fdecd77df0ad0), and `sw.js` cache `showdown-lab-release-coarsebound1` with **42 recalculated SHA256 pins** [`9cb1093`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/9cb1093335596fe65076cb520fac60fc74a9e67c); previous root and core digests verified before writes and new exact root-module RUNTIME list order checked.

Independent original [run 38686077288](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38686077288) **in progress at check**, post-integration full [38686842348](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/actions/runs/38686842348) **queued**. Do not claim either has passed yet.

### October 15 — first targeted B3 role fix on QA, plus physical melee contact reach diagnosed

**Actual QA runtime change:** Source-level evidence showed the Assassin vanish teleport used to land on the attacker's approach side, contrary to expected rear/backstab identity. In 24 matched 40/80 fighter direct V8 mirrored fixtures (three army families, seeds 1337/9917; each 240–260 logical ticks), a **direction-only** sign flip, keeping the same .075 x displacement, exact original y jitter, cooldown, HP, damage, armor, L4/L5 and targeting, raised cumulative Assassin positive damage **744 → 5,486.8**; **20/24 scenarios improved**, one-to-one corrected persistent damage-front windows summed **26 → 40**. There were negative individual front cases (8 had more simultaneous fight windows, 5 fewer), so no claim that Assassin fixes general multi-front balance. Short/censored trials do not prove win rates. Against guarded 20-fighter opponents, rear teleport did not automatically create damage; fair counterplay still needs complete seed/matchup verification.

Fixed [`3e5ac6e`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/3e5ac6e275ffc786700e1af323cf9de7d07bc0f4): compare pre-5C O(n) pressure scans and per-pair visual radii against current optimized code while preserving the currently required universal `globalBound` on BOTH control and candidate. **Six direct V8 baseline/candidate 80/120 matches with seeds 1337/7301/27919 verified identical sampled full states and end ticks after this fix.** Rerun CI needed for full release, no main merge/deploy.
- **Current role contribution measured by independent `role-multifront-baseline` CI** (run 38690830317), 60 seeded/mirrored cases with exact original-to-read-only observer digest parity. In 56 20–120 mixed/specialized cases where role exists, positive fighter-attributed aggregate HP damage (not normalized for role count/alive time): Mole 61,471.92; Dragon 33,948.28; Knight 10,027.85; Barbarian 9,729.33; Assassin 8,516.86; Sniper 8,516.05; Goose 8,271.68; Beetank 7,018.72; Goblin 4,027.9; Turtle 2,562.85. This does NOT establish which role needs a DPS buff: mitigation, guard/control and relative enrollment/time alive require correction. Persistent independently damage-producing fronts were observed in 14/14 80-fighter cases, 13/14 120-fighter cases, but only 6/14 40 and 2/14 20 with the strict corrected one-to-one persistence metric; 180/300 are censored early and must not be read as nonengaging entire matches.
- **Goblin vs heavy is next B3 controlled study:** New QA-only [`tools/qa/check-goblin-heavy-counterplay.cjs`](../tools/qa/check-goblin-heavy-counterplay.cjs), created [`f47c0d7`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/f47c0d754b47c018dcf61405c8e686d97a1931b4), per-side normalized attribution [`d31bab6`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/d31bab615f2f09f6715a427f5eb8b071fa1b1f6e), critical only-if-gap-exceeds-nominal guard [`a1dc9da`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/a1dc9da349dbe75092140a808e819fe77021166c), CI job [`fdd6fdd`](https://github.com/danickman/ShowdownLab/commit/fdd6fddb579f76bbc8db5d8b4e14e44f403ed892). The variant widens **Goblin only** when already-established visual physical collision spacing exceeds nominal `u.range/500`: `gap=visualSpacingRadius(u)+visualSpacingRadius(t)-(bodyRadius(u)+bodyRadius(t))*.22; r=gap>u.range/500?gap+.002:u.range/500`. This avoids perturbing already-viable Goblin-versus-light duels, unlike an unconditional +0.002 buffer. Original/game remains UNCHANGED; this is isolated simulation research, not a permanent Goblin buff.
- **Direct Goblin source A/B 18 checks**, 1× Goblin v Knight/Goblin/Beetank/Turtle/Dragon and 6v6/12v12 swarm targets at L1, mirrored seed 1337, 10× 239–426 logical ticks: **Knight/Goblin target full fighter-state traces exactly identical** for both variants; 1v1 versus Beetank **0→1 credited Goblin hits**, Turtle **0→1**, Dragon **0→0**; 6 Goblins vs 6 Beetanks **0→16–21 hits**, 6 Goblins vs 6 Turtles **0→17–21**, while **all heavy defenders still won** the sampled swarm matches. Dragon remained undefeated with 0 Goblin contacts at 12v12, so the geometry fix alone does not repair all combat access. Do not conclude general Goblin viability or fair swarm scaling from these short fixtures. The independent study covers L1/L4/L5 duels, 4/12/24 body swarm-versus-armored tests, seeded/mirrored 40/80/120 balanced/heavy/counter armies, defender survivor HP, cases with terminal winners and role hit contribution. Wait for its GitHub success receipt before deciding whether to ship Goblin's narrow contact fix.

### Oct 17 — user-corrected ultimate power-spike design contract
- **Design correction:** being first to unlock L4 or L5 should ordinarily **win an otherwise fair round**, with a spectacular new capability. Valid exceptions: the foe also attains L4/L5, ×2 doubling a family or overwhelming composition/numerical advantage (e.g. two/three evolved units vs fifteen strong opponents). Our previous comparisons often held many fighters L4 on both teams, so they measured **relative same-tier opportunity cost**, NOT intended asymmetric first-unlock impact. Do not treat all purposeful winner flips as unacceptable; do retain authentic damaging fronts, role counters, positive-HP accounting, full determinism and performance safety.
- New [first-to-tier design study](FIRST_ULTIMATE_TIER_SPIKE_2026-10-17.md) checks all 10 families at both rung boundaries, 20/40 populations, seed/mirror, hold/first-unlock/peer-counter/foe-×2 and 3-evolved-vs-15 exceptions: **720 genuine terminal matches and 24 read-only RAF SHA controls**. Source-only benchmark, findings **pending fresh exact-head CI**. No shipped balance changes, main or prod merge. iPhone 14 Pro Max Safari still open.
